I would like to ask Mrs Joanna Kopcińska, Mrs Anna Czech and Mr Tadeusz Dziuba, the Law and Justice Club. The question is on the implementation of the e-blood project and proceedings that are pending before the TSUE against Poland in connection with the non-implementation of the blood donation directives, and is addressed to the Ministry of Health. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u4	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On 10 December 2015, the European Commission informed that it had decided to refer a complaint to the Court of Justice of the European Union for breach of the Treaty obligations on Poland's failure to fully transpose the blood donation and blood treatment directives. Given the nature of the issues that must be regulated under Polish law, the current government has drafted a bill amending the Act on Public Blood Service and some other laws. This draft, together with the implementing acts issued on the basis of it, fully and fully implement into the Polish legal system the European Union Directive on both quality standards, safe collection and blood safety, transport rules, imports of blood and its components from outside the Member States of the European Union and the European Economic Area. Finally, the provisions of the amended law will ensure the implementation of the provisions of EU directives in Polish law, which guarantee the safety of all recipients and blood donors and personnel involved in the collection and preparation of blood or its components. They will also affect the maintenance of appropriate standards throughout the blood donation and blood treatment process, from the time the donor takes blood from the donor through its examination, preparation, transport, release and finally transfusion in the recipient. This project we are catching up on the huge arrears made by our predecessors, but this project has not yet been adopted, the procedure is ongoing. My question relates to the consequences of the European Commission's bringing an infringement of treaty obligations before the Court of Justice. Minister, please answer: What is the threat, what are the consequences of not implementing EU directives into Polish law? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u5	Thank you, Member. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health Katarzyna Głowala answers the question. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u6	Mrs. Marshalkini! High Izbo! The deadline for implementing European Union Directives 2002/98, 2004/33, 2005/61 and 2005/62 expired in 2005 and 2006. Until now, the Polish law in force has not fully implemented the above-mentioned directives. I answer the question of what is the possible continuation of the procedure. Firstly, the Court of Justice will find that we have not complied with the opinion obtained, we have not adopted the Act by 27 April 2015. This is the first sentence. If Poland does not comply with the above mentioned above. the judgment, the European Commission may refer the case to the Court of Justice again. The European Commission can then indicate the amount of the lump sum or periodic penalty payment to be paid by Poland. The second possible option: if the Court finds that the State has failed to comply with the first judgment, it may impose a lump sum or periodic penalty payment - so-called. Second sentence. In the case of Poland, for each day of the infringement, starting from the day of the second sentence, a periodic penalty payment will be charged, which will normally amount to several tens of thousands of euros per day. At the same time, I would like to inform you that the minimum lump sum is 4266 thousand. Euros. The Commission may also request that both penalties be imposed for the same infringement. As regards the calculation of the penalty, unfortunately, it is not possible to calculate precisely the penalties that threaten Poland at the moment, as the European Commission uses several coefficients for their calculation, e.g. the seriousness of the infringement and the duration of the infringement, and today it is impossible to predict what the value of these factors will be if a complaint is lodged by the European Commission. That's all.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u7	Thank you very much, Minister. The additional question is asked by Mrs Anna Czech. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u8	Thank you very much, Marshal. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Just a quick question. This draft introduces a legal basis that will enable the creation of an e-blood electronic system containing information about blood donors and adverse events. It will also serve reporting, which is very important. Can the Minister expand, clarify this subject, especially taking into account the costs of this project? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u9	I'm asking the Minister for an answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u10	Thank you. The main objective of this system is to improve and increase the supervision of blood donation and blood treatment, and thus to increase the level of blood safety and safety of blood recipients, blood donors and its components. The system is intended to replace the registers currently operating in blood donation and blood treatment centres and the Institute of Hematology and Transfusion. In addition, the e-blood system is also intended to apply to the blood treatment process at the level of medicinal entities, including hospitals and hospital blood banks. The introduction of this system, in addition to the above, will also allow the proper disposal of data concerning, for example, the storage states of blood and its components in particular blood donation and blood treatment centers, which unfortunately does not exist today. The tools provided by the e-blood system will allow for proper and contemporary tasks to be performed throughout the whole blood service system. The system is to be developed between 2017 and 2019 and the funds for its acquisition will come largely from EU funds under the Operational Programme (the "Digital Poland" Operational Programme). I would like to point out that it is today that the deadline for submitting an application to the European Commission for funding this project and today the Ministry of Digitisation, as the institution responsible for implementing the Operational Programme, will send this proposal to the European Commission. As far as the budget of the project is concerned, it is planned at 48 788 000. This is the amount resulting from the final form of the feasibility study after verification of the individual cost items. I would also like to add that the necessity of the project is due to the fact that at present there are obstacles limiting the possibility of adapting supply, i.e. blood donation, to demand, namely the use of blood and its components for medical purposes. This can be characterized as follows: blood collection data and its use are distributed in the IT systems of individual blood donation and blood treatment centres. The public blood service units use four different, independent and data-free electronic systems. Blood supply measures are carried out by individual blood donation and blood treatment centres and are not fully coordinated. At present, it is not possible to obtain information on the actual need for blood components and it is not possible to obtain information on the use of hospital requirements. Blood collection is associated with additional inconveniences for the donor. The donors see the need and the ability to limit them. Therefore, the Ministry of Health [[♪ The bell ♪]] has decided to introduce this system. Thank you very much.

Both the completion of individual Border Guard facilities and departments, as well as their equipment, allow for the full implementation of statutory tasks in the field of state border protection. In order to prevent criminal offences, the state border shall be continuously monitored using various modern means. The systematic provision of border services with more and better tools to combat border crime increases the effectiveness of this formation both at and outside border crossing points. Common patrols with border officers of neighbouring countries, including at the external border of the European Union, including, of course, the state border with Ukraine, are one of the important forms of state border protection. Cooperation with the border guards of Ukraine, including the readmission process, is an important element of this assurance. I would also like to emphasise this aspect of the matter. Information provision and exchange activities shall be carried out, inter alia. through mutual contacts between border guards and border services of neighbouring countries. They rely on the daily exchange of statistical data and the transmission of any information likely to affect the protection of the state border or the fluidity of border traffic. This cooperation shall be subject to ongoing evaluation and correction during meetings within the framework of the border representative's apparatus. As far as the benefits of building a base in Redzików are concerned, the region will certainly achieve such benefits. We assume that the base staff will make extensive use of the social service base. Local workers should find employment in the database as well as in the construction of the database. In order to familiarize Polish entrepreneurs with the possibilities to participate in tenders, meetings are also organised during the industrial days, where opportunities concerning the development of the region are presented. As far as funding is concerned, the National Health Fund already a few years ago developed an algorithm setting the financial rate for the daily readiness of a hospital emergency unit, which consists of two elements. The first element is infrastructure. This is where the equipment is assessed, which is the minimum necessary laid down in the regulation of the Minister of Health, including the possession of a landing site. The second element of this algorithm is related to functionality, i.e. the number of staff, the number of patients who are admitted there. The shift in assessment, including an algorithm of any element, results in a change in the daily readiness rate, which is determined for at least one year. Therefore, if it is amended, the lifted landing requirement, on the basis of an algorithm that has been in force for perhaps 4 or 5 years, it may appear that the daily readiness rate will be minimally lower. On the other hand, this does not prejudice this change absolutely, because the rescue department can increase its valuation by increasing the number of benefits provided to patients. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u236	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Communications with Poles abroad and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, I have the honour to present a report on the Commission's draft law amending the Polish Charter Act and some other laws, print No 157. Ladies and gentlemen, the amendment of the Polish Charter Act, which we initiated in this term of office, had three main points, aimed at introducing three fundamental changes. Firstly, it introduced a revolution when it comes to thinking about this law. Let us remind you that in 2007, when this law was passed, it was intended mainly for our compatriots in the East, who live all the time on the former borders of I and II of the Republic of Poland and do not intend to come to Poland permanently. The bill was designed to help them maintain contact with the matrix. Among other things, they were subject to solutions such as facilitating the receipt of visas, exemption from tuition fees in Poland or discounts in terms of public transport or admission to museums. In this amendment, we are facing also those Poles in the East who want to come to Poland and settle in our country permanently. For the first time in history, we give our compatriots a choice: if you want to live where your fathers, grandparents, lived, we will help you to maintain Polishness outside the country, but if you want to come to the matrices, we also give you this opportunity and support you, we give you the opportunity to settle. In practice, it would look like that the holder of the Polish Charter, who comes to Poland with the intention to settle here permanently, receives a permanent residence card free of charge from the machine, then receives Polish citizenship after a year and at that time can count on various kinds of help during this most difficult period of adaptation. The second important change that we are introducing in this amendment is the possibility of obtaining the Pole's Charter not so much as so far, only in the consulates of the Republic of Poland, where the issuing authority was a consul, but also in voivodeship offices, where the issuing authority would be a voivode. This is particularly important for citizens of those countries where there is no full democracy, such as Belarus, where local authorities are reluctant to look at the holders of the Polish Charter and applicants for this document, where the authorities have introduced a law which some professional groups prohibit from applying for the Polish Charter. Thanks to this amendment, our compatriots, coming from Belarus to Poland, will be able to submit documents and then collect the Polish Charter. The third very important point of this amendment, which needs to be mentioned, is the change of thought and the change of approach to our fellow countrymen in the East, who have the Polish Charter, but do not have Polish citizenship. We are introducing a provision whereby our compatriots in extraordinarily situations, in situations where their health or life is at risk, will be able to be treated on principles similar to those of Polish citizens, with respect for international law, of course. We want to give a very clear signal that, for us, for the present government, for the present parliament, our compatriots outside the country, whether or not they have Polish citizenship, are very, very important and will be given priority. During the work of the committee and the subcommittee, some amendments were made, which were voted by the committees. I'll limit myself to mentioning some of the most important things. If there are questions later, it will be necessary, then I will develop the issues of individual changes. The first change in relation to the project originally submitted consists in a change in the scope of benefits. Here we collided on the one hand with a certain financial reality, on the other hand - with concerns discussed among others. by the boundary environments. We originally wanted a person who came to Poland with the intention to settle here permanently, to have the opportunity to obtain in the first year of his/her stay, i.e. during this most difficult adaptation period, similar assistance received by people coming to our country as part of the process of repatriation. The border environments pointed to the threat that the creation of such a strong incentive, such large facilities would lead to a kind of drainage from Poles of the borders of the I and II Republic of Poland, while after analyses carried out in agreement with the government, it turned out that such rich benefits would exceed the possibilities of the Polish state budget. That is why we introduced a change in the committee's work, which is that every person who wants to settle in Poland in this way, obtain Polish citizenship, during the first 3 months of his stay will be able to count on a monthly financial allowance of 50% minimum wage, and for the next 6 months it will be 60% that amount. To put it simply, any person who wants to settle in Poland in this mode will be able to count on the average of PLN 600 to be co-financed for 9 months. On the one hand, it is not a big amount, but on the other hand for people who come here and have no support at the start, it will undoubtedly be a big help. The second important change we are making is due to the concern for the budget. This is the introduction of a mechanism which will allow to control expenditure incurred by the Polish state's budget during the implementation of the Polish Charter Act. These benefits, which I just mentioned, will be implemented from the special special special reserve created for the implementation of the Polish Charter Act. We, on the other hand, introduce the possibility that the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, who monitors the use of this reserve, sees that the amount of the special-purpose reserve is used in 75% , he will be able to take action to stop the payment of benefits. What does that mean? This means that our compatriots will still be able to apply for a permanent residence card, will receive it and after a year they will receive Polish citizenship if they wish, and after using the amount included in the special reserve of the Polish state budget they will not be able to receive the benefit. Another important change concerns the voivodship offices in which you will be able to apply for the Polish Charter. Originally we thought it would be possible to do it in all voivodships. Later, after analysing the situation, possible expectations, needs and possibilities of infrastructure and administrative capacity, we came to the conclusion that it is necessary to introduce a provision that the Council of Ministers will be able to indicate the regional offices and those voivodships who will be able to issue the Polish Charter. The Council of Ministers will also be able to identify the countries whose citizens will be able to apply for the Polish Charter at these regional offices. Another important change, which is also dictated by an analysis of the current situation, i.e. an analysis of how the IT infrastructure is currently operating, which is created for the functioning of the Polish Charter Act - it is also about the financial possibilities of the Polish state's budget - concerns the period of vacathio legis. Namely, we propose that the law, regulations concerning settling here, receiving a permanent residence card enter into force 3 months from the date of publication of the law, while the provisions which will concern the competence of the voivodships, i.e. the possibility of applying for the Polish Charter in the voivodeship offices, and the benefits which will be paid to people who wish to settle in Poland in this manner, are to enter into force only from 1 January 2017, i.e. about 5 months later. It must be said that the committee has voted relatively in agreement with these changes, although it has not been without discussion. Three minority proposals were also submitted. I will present these proposals to you, and they are relatively short. The first proposal concerns Chapter 2b: Form and method of providing support to the holder of the Polish Charter granted for an indefinite period. It is proposed that Article. 8k was as follows: I'm sorry, I'm sorry, I'm sorry, I'm sorry, I'm sorry, I'm sorry, but I'm sorry. The Pole's card granted for an indefinite period entitles its holder to receive financial assistance of one tenth of the average monthly salary in the Republic of Poland in the sector of enterprises without rewards from the profit announced by the Central Statistical Office for the month of December preceding the year in which the aid is paid. 2. Aid referred to in paragraph 1. 1, is an annual, one-off payment in local currency after calculated from Polish zlotys according to the NBP average rate from the date of submission of the application. The aid shall be paid by a consul or an authorised consular staff member. 3. The Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs shall, by means of a regulation, determine the procedure, manner and time limits for the submission of applications, taking into account the need for efficient and rapid handling. This is the minority amendment tabled by Mr Gryglas. This amendment... The intention is absolutely right, because it comes down to paying some benefits to our elderly people, for example. for the purchase of medicines or municipal fees. We know that pensions in countries such as Belarus, Ukraine and Russia are very low, so it would certainly be useful to do so, but also to be responsible - after the conversions, which show that this solution in the version proposed by your Member would go about 20% the functioning of this law - the committee recommended this amendment negatively. The second amendment concerns Article. 1. In point 9 (a), point (b) is replaced by the following: b should read as follows: 4 is replaced by the following: 4. The Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs shall determine, by means of a regulation: 1. the way in which the certificates referred to in paragraph 1 are issued. 3 point 7, 2. the model certificate referred to in paragraph. 3 point 7, 3. a list of Polish or Polish organisations entitled to issue the certificates referred to in paragraph. 3 (7) taking into account the need to ensure the reliability of the certificates issued and taking into account the scope of information on the activities of the applicant, which certify its commitment to the language and culture of the Polish or Polish national minority. Ladies and gentlemen, the intention of this amendment is to seal the possibility, so I interpret it, granting the Polish Charter to people who do not have documents confirming their Polish origin. I would like to remind you that the provision which introduced the possibility of receiving the Polish Charter on the basis of a certificate issued by a Polish organization operating in the East came from the fact that some of our compatriots in Soviet times, during the 20th interwar period, were subjected to very strong repression: exports to Kazakhstan from Soviet Ukraine in 1936, or a Polish operation in the Soviet Union, where 111 thousand were shot. Poles were only because they were Poles, and several tens of thousands were sent to the camps. These and other traumatic events led to some of our compatriots knowingly destroying all documents confirming their Polish origin. And for these people it is precisely this way of obtaining the Polish Charter. It is worth noting that at the moment it is not so that any Polish organization can issue a certificate. This organisation, which is indicated in the Regulation of the President of the Council of Ministers, shall, at the request of the Minister for Foreign Affairs, be entitled to issue such certificates. For example, in Ukraine there are two such Polish organizations, in Belarus one. In our view, there should be no additional sharpening and restrictions. This could be limited to introducing only more bureaucracy and would not contribute to improving the functioning of this law. Therefore, the committee has strongly recommended this amendment. Mr Gryglas is also the author of the amendment. This is just presented as a minority proposal. And a third minority motion. Well, there's a proposal in the article. 1 in point 9 (a) ♪ to read as follows: paragraph shall be added. The following 8 are added: 8. For applicants who have not demonstrated their knowledge of the Polish language as provided for in paragraph 1. 7, evaluates the knowledge of the Polish language during the conversation of the consuls, and in the case referred to in Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Four, voivode. The assessment of the knowledge of the Polish language of the consul or the voivode shall be carried out in accordance with the European system of certification of foreign languages for level A1 (the lowest basic language). This was the proposal made by Mrs Fabisiak. The intention is understandable, and the wording of the amendment is unacceptable because this provision would require consuls and voivods to de facto assess the level of language skills for which these officials have no competence or authority. Hence, such an action could be questioned in a very simple way. In addition, today at the committee meeting we heard from a representative of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs that this provision is contrary to the applicable rules at all. That is why the committee has recommended this amendment negatively and I have just presented it as a minority motion. Ladies and gentlemen! The Commission adopted all the amendments unanimously. I hope that the further legislative process will also take place in a similar, compatible and compatible atmosphere. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u282	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Labour Code Act contained in Seym Printing No 298. Designed changes in the Labour Code aim to limit the so-called. The first-day syndrome of hiring workers illegally without a written employment contract. Although the Labour Code now requires that a contract be concluded in writing, if it is not concluded in such a form, but by oral or implied means, the employer should confirm to the employee in writing the findings on the basic terms and conditions of employment. This confirmation should include all elements that have been previously agreed between the employee and the employer and which should also include a contract of employment. It should be provided to the staff member at the latest on the day on which he starts his work, i.e. by the end of his first day of work. The State Labour Inspection submits that in practice, this regulation serves employers for the illegal employment of workers, because in the case of the labour inspector's control, the rule is to explain to employers that workers do not yet have employment contracts, because this is their first day of work on which they have been admitted to work, and written confirmation will be provided to workers by the end of their working day, in accordance with the current Article. 29 § 2 of the Labour Code. According to the 2014 Report on the Activities of the State Labour Inspection, illegal employment consisting of entrusting work without written confirmation of the type of employment contract, its terms and conditions and the failure to notify the person employed or performing any other employment for social security was found in 18% of the persons controlled. In 2013, this percentage was lower in 2012, unfortunately increasing. These irregularities concerned 6.2% persons under control. In the opinion of labour inspectors, entrepreneurs entrust work illegally because circumvention of legislation is relatively easy. Employing in the grey zone is conducive to leaky regulation. This applies in particular to the deadlines for written confirmation to the employee of the employment contract - the day of starting work - and the application for social security - here is a seven-day period - which in practice allows work without fulfilling these obligations for a long time. Although the failure to confirm in writing the employment contract concluded with the employee is punishable by a fine of 1 000. PLN up to 30,000. zł, however, in practice with the current regulation contained in Article. 29 § 2 Labour Code The Labour Inspector often cannot impose a fine on the employer on the basis of this provision, when he suspects that, contrary to the employer's claim, the employee is not at all the first day at work. The illegal employment of workers is not only detrimental to workers but also to employers who employ workers legally because they have higher labour costs and are therefore less competitive than less honest employers, and to the state budget, which is suffering losses due to the impact of taxes and insurance premiums. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In connection with such practices, we submit to the High House a draft law which provides for the introduction into the Labour Code of the order that the employer should confirm the basic arrangements relating to the conclusion of a contract of employment in a form other than written form before the employee is admitted to work. Having a written employment contract or a written confirmation of the basic arrangements relating to the conclusion of a non-written employment contract before admission to work will make it easier for employees to investigate their benefits and rights from the employment relationship, as well as to benefit from protection guaranteed by the provisions of the Social Insurance Act. The aim of the project is also to provide the State Labour Inspection with a more effective tool to ensure the effectiveness of controls on the legal employment of workers. Such a change will enable labour inspectors to identify illegal employment of workers whenever, in the case of checks, it appears that work in the context of employment relationships is carried out by persons with whom written employment contracts have not been concluded in advance and some have not been written down in advance of the basic conditions of employment. A material standard imposing an obligation on an employer should be accompanied by a standard to be imposed in the event of a breach of that obligation. It is therefore proposed to clarify that the fine provided for will be subject to non-confirmation in writing of the employment contract concluded with the employee before he is admitted to work. It is also proposed to make two other amendments related to the revision of the Article. 29 § 2 of the Labour Code. They aim to harmonise the nomenclature used in the Code of Labour related to the employer's obligations, which must be fulfilled before the employee is admitted to work, inter alia. familiarise the worker with the working regulations and the young worker with a list of light works. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to stress that this proposal was discussed at the Trouble Group meeting. Labour law of the Social Dialogue Council on 19 January this year. The social side of this team adopted a positive attitude towards the project, recommending the government side to extend the period of vacathio legis law to 6 weeks from the originally designed 14 days. The recommendation was accepted by the government. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Please refer the draft amendment to the Labour Code in print No 298 for further work in the Sejm Committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

That's why it's like this. also for safety, so that the worker who goes to work has this security, the certainty that this agreement will be signed, that he will not work for many months in black. I believe that this is a very good proposal to change, and that is why on behalf of my own Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I request that this project be directed to further work in the [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Extraordinary Commission for Changes in Codifications. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u285	Thank you very much. Ms Katarzyna Mrzygłock, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u286	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present an opinion on the government's draft amendments to the Labour Code presented in Seym printing No 298. High Izbo! In the explanatory memorandum to this project, we read that its main objective is to reduce the grey area. Unfortunately, this phenomenon still seems to be a big problem for our labour market. There is a social right for these types of practices, which are one of the elements of unfair competition. The Civic Platform supports any action limiting the grey area, but we have some comments on this project. The fact is that now the conclusion of a written agreement and the notification of this fact to the relevant institutions may take place at the end of the first day and can indeed lead to a situation where such an alleged first day takes a week, a month or more until the State Labour Inspection checks. However, we need to realise that unfair practices take place by mutual agreement. Of course, we can ask ourselves who has more to gain or lose on such a deal. We can argue that young people taking up first jobs are easier to manipulate. However, the facts are that today it is enough for a commonly used mobile phone to be able to document the fact of doing work. Thus, it seems that the actual problem does not apply to the relevant documentation, but rather to the employee's willingness to disclose the unfair behaviour of the employer. It is worth considering, therefore, whether the government's proposal that an employer should be required to create a preliminary document confirming employment before a person takes up new duties is indeed a solution to the problem we are talking about. After all, it is not difficult to enter a date into such a document only at the time of inspection. Unfair collusion will still be difficult to prove. I would like to remind you that the provisions that this project is about to change were designed primarily for companies with a complex organizational structure, having a headquarters far away from the workplace of employees or entrepreneurs using the services of external accounting companies. Under the law in emergency situations, economic operators have the possibility of hiring a new employee quickly. We can imagine what kind of emergency situations this is all about. This is, for example, a abandonment of work or a sudden illness and a necessary immediate replacement. It is interesting to note that in 2014 nearly 1/5 companies controlled by the State Labour Inspection were exposed to employees without any documentation. All of these cases seem unlikely to be the result of these emergencies. As I mentioned earlier, there is still a consent to the functioning of the grey zone in our country, and perhaps we should focus primarily on educational activities in order to change this state. On the other hand, with regard to the concrete ad hoc action proposed by the government, the Civic Platform believes that this proposal should be further discussed, in particular by examining opinions and listening to our social partners. We therefore request that the project be referred to the committee for further work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u289	Thank you very much, Member. Ms Joanna Augustynowska, the Modern Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u290	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The main objective of the draft law amending the Labour Code Act is to introduce an obligation for the employer to conclude a contract of employment in writing before the employee is admitted to work. To date, in accordance with the Article. 29 § 2 Labour Code This obligation existed on the day of starting work, which means that the employer had time to sign the contract until the end of the first day of the employee's work. The rules in this form leave a wide scope for abuse and employment in the grey area and effectively hinder the State Labour Inspection process of checking the legality of employment. The rule is to explain to employers that the day on which the legality of employment is controlled is the first day of the worker's work. This is the so-called minister mentioned by you. First-day syndrome. This situation is unacceptable, especially since circumvention is relatively easy, widespreadly exploited and employment in the shadow economy creates large disparities in the labour market. The proposed amendment to the Labour Code aims to eliminate the pathologies mentioned above and, first, to enhance the protection of workers' rights, second, to facilitate the investigation of workers' benefits and rights from employment relationships, thirdly, also to benefit from the protection guaranteed by social security rules and to make the State Labour Inspection more effective tools to ensure the effectiveness of checks on the legal employment of workers. During the legislative process, the project was sent to the social partners - both employees' representatives, e.g. trade unions, as well as employers' organisations, e.g. Confederation of Leviatan, Employers' Union Business Centre Club or Employers of the Republic of Poland. And so, sequentially: The Confederation of Leviathan indicates that the direction of the proposed changes is correct, albeit incomplete, because it does not take into account possible administrative problems in large companies with a complex organizational structure, as well as when the keeping of the company's personnel and accounting records is passed on to third parties. Business Centre Club, on the other hand, believes that these changes, although they do not affect too much of the regulation of the Labour Code, can significantly improve the safety of workers both in the labour market and within the context of the employment relationship itself. The Polish employers also point out that the direction of change is right, but they signal, like Leviathan, that administrative problems can arise both in large companies, which will have to reorganise their staff systems and in small companies, where the formalities relating to employment are carried out by the owner, and I remind you that the burdens imposed on employers before the employee is admitted to work, i.e. the obligation to send for health and safety training, to carry out research on occupational medicine, are the burdens that employers most often complain about, which hinder flexibility in the labour market. These opinions were also very critical. For example, the Association of Entrepreneurs and Employers assesses the project as harmful and unnecessarily stiffening labour law standards, indicating that the proposed provision is in no way consistent with the economic reality, particularly in the case of industries where there are often sudden staff shortages necessary for immediate completion. In the opinion of the Supreme Court, too, it raises doubts about the new wording of the article. Point 281 (2) of the Labour Code is not appropriate to change the wording of Article. 104 § 2 Labour Code and Art. 200 § 5 of the Labour Code. In both cases, the need to fulfil the information obligation prior to the commencement of work, which, according to the Supreme Court, is synonymous with the admission to work, so even with regard to the obligation to familiarise the youth worker with the list of light works, the employer must not hide the claim that he is to fulfil these obligations on the day of commencement of work. There is therefore no need to modify these regulations. That's what the Supreme Court's opinion is about. The positive effect of these changes and these provisions of the Act will be the benefits of the inclusion of workers in the public system, including the possibility of using public health services, as well as the inclusion of social security systems - pensions and pensions. In the case of code agreements, the benefits for workers will also be covered by the rights resulting from the employment relationship, inter alia. paid holiday or sickness benefits. In view of the fact that the draft amendment is a project that is going in the right direction in order to protect workers' rights, the Modern will support further work in the committee, but we suggest that the Supreme Court's opinions be taken into account in this work because they are well founded. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u291	Thank you very much. Mr Krystian Jarubas from the club of the Polish People's Party will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u292	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I am pleased to present my position towards the government draft law amending the Labour Code Act, print No 298. Ladies and gentlemen! The subject of the project is the amendment of the Article. 29 § 2 of the Labour Code. This provision currently provides that a contract of employment is to be concluded in writing. If the employment contract has not been concluded in writing, the employer should, at the latest on the day on which the employee starts working, confirm to the employee in writing the arrangements concerning the parties to the contract, the type of contract and its terms and conditions. The draft provides for the amendment of the Article. 29 § 2 of the Labour Code imposing an obligation on the employer to confirm the employee in writing the arrangements for the parties to the contract, the type of contract and its conditions prior to the admission of the worker to work where the employment contract has not been concluded in writing. Ladies and gentlemen, other changes to the provisions of the Article also deserve to be accepted. 104 § 2, Article 200 § 5 and Art. 281 point 2 of the Labour Code. It is understandable that the government is carrying out the announcements of the President of this government in the person of the Prime Minister and is trying to report the projects relevant to the line set out in the exposé. A worker who has at least one child up to 14 years of age shall be exempt from 16 hours or 2 days of work during a calendar year, with the right to remuneration and reading this hypothesis together with Article. 189 Labour Code that if both parents or guardians of a child are employed, this entitlement shall be granted to one of them. The choice that parents or carers will use depends on the stakeholders themselves. There is also no obstacle to parents or carers sharing the right to this exemption and each of them enjoying one day off, but the exemption is independent of the number of children. Going further - this is also about building family policies, in order to increase birth rates, even if the 500+ programme, which moves significantly in prima aprilis - I would like to draw attention to this state of affairs and show the lack of justice and discrimination in our legal system of families greater than 2+1. On the other hand, as regards the proposed amendment itself, I find that this is an appropriate legislative procedure and, with a slight amendment of the existing regulations, it will increase the security of workers in the context of the establishment of a employment relationship which, assessed as an obligation of the worker to provide work to the employer at the place and time specified by him, under his direction, as well as the obligation of the employer to pay the appropriate remuneration, is checked by the State Labour Inspection inspectors, with all the consequences of its existence or non-existence. This change should therefore be considered appropriate and worthy of being introduced in the context of labour rights and labour law certainty in general. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will support further work on the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u306	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The purpose of this draft law is to amend the Law of 12 March 2012 on the tax on the extraction of certain fossils and concerns the suspension of its collection in the period from 1 April 2016 to 31 December 2017. But before I move on to the justification for this change, it is worth recalling that the 2012 Act was introduced at a time when Poland and other countries were struggling with the economic crisis. It was therefore reasonable for the State, as owner of natural resources, to transfer some of the revenue from these deposits to the public finance sector. It has not been and is not a novelty since such taxation is widely used in countries with such deposits. The price of copper and silver on the world markets was also important at the time, because it has a significant impact on the situation of the copper and silver producer, i.e. KGHM Polska Miedź. Thus, during the period of preparation and introduction of this tax, the average price of red metal in 2012 was at USD 7950 per tonne and in 2011 its price was record - almost 10,000. A dollar a ton. According to the reports prepared by the Ministry of Finance, the financing of this tax did not threaten the company until 2014. There was no decrease in employment, no change in remuneration conditions, no closure of any branch of the company, all planned investments were carried out, and even despite the introduction of the tax in 2012 at the turn of 2012 and 2013, KGHM's share quotations reached their historical maximum. The situation deteriorated when the price of copper and silver fell dramatically in 2015 and reached 4 000. A dollar a ton. The same decrease in prices was also noted by the second of the metals subject to tax, i.e. silver. And it is precisely this fall in prices, at the same time forecasting that in 2 years the price of red metal can reach 8,000. USD per tonne justifies the proposal to amend the Mining Tax Act in such a way as to suspend the tax obligation in question for the time of the price depression of copper and silver, because such a continuing decrease in the prices of raw materials subject to that tax will not only result in lower revenue to the budget, but also have very serious consequences for copper and silver miners, namely KGHM. It may result in a decrease in the profitability of current production, particularly dangerous for KGHM branches, where the situation is much worse than the overall situation of the company. I am referring to the Mining Works Lubin, which systematically declines profitability. It may also result in a decrease in the profitability of the planned investments, the failure to implement them, and thus the reduction of future copper extraction in Poland, the abandonment of investments in the vicinity of Bolesławica. It can also increase debt and reduce the development opportunities of copper and silver miners from Polish deposits due to the increasingly poor conditions of extraction. I mean deposits in Jakubów, Głogów Deep, because these deposits are low, there is a high temperature, there are also gases, such as hydrogen sulphide, which significantly increase copper ore extraction. Therefore, the period of suspension of this tax will be an excellent time to analyse the formula for charging this tribute, often criticised not only by the managers of the company, but also by scientists and specialists in this field. It will also be a good time to introduce changes that will allow KGHM Polska Miedź to operate and develop safely. Perhaps it is worth, as it is in other countries, taxing the result, i.e. profit, and not, as it is applied on the basis of a functioning law, the amount of extracted but not sold raw material, because the time from extraction to sale is usually a period from 1 month to 2 months. This is today, as you can see, the right time, as it has also since this year introduced taxation on other institutions, such as banks, insurance companies. So it is worth resting the company that helped and supported the state budget in difficult times. High Izbo! An important aspect is also the financial impact, which due to low prices of copper and silver will not allow the implementation of budgetary plans and will oscillate at the level of PLN 500 million. In the budget, let me remind you, the amount of PLN 1.5 billion has been written down. This would be possible if the price of copper were to be at a level of 6 and 7 000. A dollar a ton, and that's not how it is today, as we know. The fall in the budgetary impact will therefore be balanced by the increase in the value of KGHM's shares. The suspension of this tax will increase KGHM's investment, which will increase the indirect revenue of the state budget, as well as local authorities, which are suffering from post-captive effects in their territories. This bill goes against the announcements of Prime Minister Beata Szydło, who announced in Lubin on 6 October 2015 the introduction of a law eliminating this tax, which she assessed as unfair and harmful as from 1 January 2016. However, we, the applicant, with a view to the security of the state budget, propose the suspension of this tax, its verification and perhaps a revision of the formula used. We give time to make good decisions and good changes. We are convinced that you will support our proposal. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u312	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the Parliamentary draft law amending the law on the extraction of certain minerals, print No 301. The draft law notified by the Civic Platform aims to suspend the collection of fossil tax until 31 December 2017. The fossil tax law entered into force in April 2012, 4 years ago, and introduced a tax obligation for entities operating in the field of copper and silver mining. The price of copper and silver on world markets has a fundamental impact on tax levels. Since 2011, the world markets have had very high prices for these raw materials. At that time the average price of copper was almost 10,000. USD per tonne and 2012 - almost 8,000. A dollar a ton. Digging is a national good and at very favourable prices can be a source of revenue for the state budget. This gave rise to the introduction of a tax on some fossils. However, in the following years prices for these raw materials systematically decreased and now the price of copper reaches only half of the price recorded in 2012. The same is true of the price of silver. While a mechanism is used to determine the impact of raw material prices on the amount of raw materials, this mechanism is used to determine the tax. does not reduce sufficiently significant price fluctuations. In such a situation, the amount of concentrate produced or the amount of copper and silver produced in the product is increasingly affected by taxes. During the legislative procedure preceding the introduction of the fossil tax, the statutory requirement for regular monitoring of the financial impact of the tax on the financial condition of economic operators in this area was adopted. Each year, the Ministry of Finance conducted such analyses for the Sejm. They pointed to a decrease in the profitability of entrepreneurs who increasingly raised this problem. The fossil tax case was heavily used in the last election campaign. In the current situation of a significant fall in the price of silver and copper, but at the same time promising, as I would like to stress, the global forecasts announcing a rapid increase in the prices of these raw materials, the best solution is to suspend the collection of the tax and re-examine the appropriateness of its continued application or to change the formula of this tax. For industry players, this will be the time to increase investment before the new price economy. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u316	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the law on the extraction of certain minerals. The Kukiz Club15 generally calls for simple, low and fair taxes. Any tax reduction initiative should be analysed taking into account these three criteria. Unfortunately, the initiative of the Civic Platform Group does not meet them because it creates uncertainty about state policy in this important area of the national economy. The suspension of tax collection is not contrary to the principles of tax law, nor does it affect European Union law, including competition law, but creates an atmosphere of temporary and chaos legislative solutions for the industry. The legislative quality of the project, which appears rather to be part of political marketing, raises concerns. The content of the draft law has been limited to an absolute minimum, thereby violating the principles of correct legislation. The need to regulate, among others, has been omitted. the reporting obligation referred to in Article. 14 law, registration obligation as described in Article. 15 and 16 laws and statutory delegations referred to, inter alia, in Section VII of the Act. In the justification of the project we read that the revenue from the tax on the extraction of certain fossils amounts to about PLN 1.1.5 billion per year. This is more than 0.5% annual revenue from taxes. The suspension of the tax obligation will have a significant impact on the state of public finances and the loss will have to be completed. Applicants do not propose new sources of financing for the deficit. The indication of an increase in the value of KGHM's shares does not imply a higher revenue of the state budget. Moreover, there is no basis for an increase in investment expenditure by KGHM during the period under consideration. The explanatory memorandum lacks a reliable analysis of the economic situation of the entity addressed by the amendment of the law. In 2015, the Council KGHM recorded 2788 million losses at unit level and PLN 5012 million losses at consolidated level, but one year earlier this was PLN 2414 million and PLN 2450 million profit respectively. The Minister of Treasury pointed out the need for an audit in KGHM Polska Miedź SA, which is to show, among others. the reasons for the investment problems carried out by the company in Chile. The company's management confirmed the start of an audit, the results of which are expected at the end of April and May. Thus, the parliamentary initiative is at least premature if we do not know the precise situation of the company. In addition to the field of substantive considerations on the principles of correct legislation and economics, the question of justifying the Ironic Law with reference to the electoral promises made by the Prime Minister. The current political fight can be conducted during a public debate, among others. during the discussion phase of the draft law, but the political arguments in this discussion should not be transferred to the content of the justification of the law. As a basis for consideration in this regard, it should be pointed out that the fossil tax is one of the basic instruments for the participation of the whole nation in the raw materials resources of the country, and that giving up its collection bears the mark of expropriation of the nation from its rights. Manipulating taxes without a clear justification of national interest violates the principles of social justice. The fossil tax was introduced by the PO-PSL government in 2012, which the applicants do not seem to remember. In order to justify its introduction, a representative of the Minister of Finance of the previous government stated that, here I recall, there is no justification for the huge profits of the copper sector to keep the government at a low level to date. Other countries rich in raw materials are fully aware of the need to participate in the benefits of non-renewable fossils, which they have been doing for many years. The Treasury, and therefore, the general public of the Republic of Poland, has exclusive ownership of the deposits used, so it has the full right to carry out the benefits associated with them for the general good. End of speech. As a general rule, this statement should be accepted. It should be noted that chaotic legislative initiatives, especially in the area of taxation, create turbulence for investors with additional political, administrative and economic risks, which should be avoided at all costs. The duration of the mining projects is several dozen times longer than the period of suspension of the tax proposed in the draft law. The introduction of such ephemeral solutions may have a negative impact on the assessment of the viability of long-term mining projects. In view of the above, Kukiz15 does not support the bill submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u318	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The tax, which concerns one company from my region, the Lubinian company KGHM, has been levied for several years since 18 April 2012, which is in fact since the entry into force of the Law of 2 March 2012 on the extraction of certain fossils. In practice, revenues were made at a much lower level. The elimination of the tax, as is estimated today, is basically 500 million for the budget, and this is mainly due to the fall in raw material prices. The situation of the copper and silver producers is significantly influenced by the prices of this raw material on world markets. Copper was the most expensive one in 2011 when it cost nearly 10,000 at the London Stock Exchange. Dollars. On the other hand, in 2012, when the tax was introduced, the average price was already at 8 000. A dollar a ton. Currently, the price of this metal slightly exceeds 4 000. A dollar a ton. I would like to mention that the same price decrease is noted by the second of the metals subject to the tax obligation, i.e. silver. Its prices compared to prices from the period when the tax on certain fossils was introduced also decreased by half. Such a decrease in the prices of raw materials subject to tax on certain minerals caused not only lower revenues to the budget, but also a drastic decrease in the profitability of businesses operating in the field of extraction of raw materials covered by the tax obligation. The fossil tax scale has a really complex construction. I will risk saying that this is the most complicated tax scale in the Polish tax system. The result of its technical assumptions is a higher tax increase compared to the profit increase which is an economic subject of the tax. This phenomenon occurs with varying degrees depending on the market price of copper. The increase in the price of copper generates a higher profit for the company, but the increase in profit is lower than the increase in tax. In September last year, when she was in Lubin during the campaign, she promised to abolish this tax and boasted that she had a bill ready. Today, according to reports, the Ministry of Finance is preparing its reduction by 1/3. The Civic Platform, referring to this promise, has prepared a bill leading to the failure to collect tax for less than 2 years. At that time, in the view of the applicants, an analysis should be undertaken in order to establish a new formula for this tax. From the perspective of the taxpayer, many negative aspects of this tax can be mentioned: the high share of fixed costs, the need for continuous recovery of production capacity, the need to provide financing for the strategic investment programme over a period of 3 and 5 years, the need to carry out underlying transactions, hedges, the need to stabilise revenues for up to 3 years and to ensure the sustainability of the financing of the investment programme. Many negative aspects can be mentioned. Unfortunately, there are not many positive aspects. The advantage of the proposed solution is above all that this form of tribute is more socially acceptable than any further increase in indirect taxes. I believe that this tax should never really be introduced. Therefore, the Modern will support measures to fully withdraw from collecting the tax on certain minerals. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u322	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on the Tax on the Extraction of Certain Copies, according to print No 301. The subject of the project is the amendment of the Law of 12 March 2012 on the tax on the extraction of certain minerals. The scope of the amendment concerns the suspension of the levy on copper and silver extraction from 1 April this year. Until 31 December 2017, i.e. for a period of 21 months. In their explanatory memorandum, the developers suggest that this would be an appropriate period to examine the rules for charging this tax and to make some changes to the current system of charging, while at the same time it would be time to stabilise the financial situation of KGHM Polska Miedź, whose profitability rates have fallen dramatically in recent years. The projectors also point out that they meet the intentions of the ruling party, Prime Minister Beata Szydło, who announced the elimination of this unfair and harmful tax at all. The law on this subject, which was adopted by the High House in 2012, resulted, and this does not change, from the general assumption that natural resources, fossils, including copper and silver, belong to national wealth, are the property of all Poles and not only to regional good. Before the entry into force of the Act, the mining plants paid only the operating fee and property tax to local authorities, without being paid to the State budget. The tax on minerals, copper and silver resulted from some calculations, which took into account both the amount of copper and silver in the produced concentrate or in the ore of copper and silver, as well as the price of these raw materials on a given day recorded on the London Stock Exchange. The applicable rule is therefore simple: the more copper and silver in the product or in the concentrate and the higher the price of these raw materials, the higher the tax, and, of course, the other way around. At the time of the adoption of the Act in 2012, the net profit in KGHM was around the amount of PLN 8 billion, with a good level of remuneration for the crew, and the tax due at that time amounted to around PLN 1.8 billion. Today, with the drop in prices of both copper and silver, the tax according to the applicants of the project will amount to about PLN 500 million. The High Sejm also imposed an obligation to analyse periodically the situation caused by the current tax and submit this assessment to the High House. After 2 years - or, in fact, after 1.5 years - the functioning of the Act the Minister of Finance made such an assessment and it did not cross out the essence of the tax itself. At present, such an assessment should be re-evaluated for the proposed amendments to the original law. Then we would have a documented view as to whether this tax has harmed mining plants so much, or whether there are other reasons. Maybe it was the wrong investments that caused KGHM's financial condition to decrease? However, I would like to draw attention to the fact that, in a particular way, it is important that the economics are the least clean policy that seems dominant in this case. To sum up, High Izbo, knowing that the first reading of the bill does not prejudge its final shape, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I am in favour of transferring the project to further work in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u339	The Marshal of the Sejm, after consulting the Bureau of the Sejm, proposes that the Sejm refer to the Parliament's draft law amending the Act on the Tax on the Extraction of Certain Copies, contained in print No 301, to the Committee on Public Finance for consideration.
