We proceed with the examination of item 17 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Public Procurement Law and some other laws (print No 366). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development of Mr Mariusz Haladyj to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u340	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment to the Public Procurement Law aims primarily at implementing two European directives in 2014, i.e. so-called. the Classical Directive and the Sectoral Directive. This amendment is at the same time the first stage of the transformation of the public procurement system into a strong instrument for the implementation of the economic and social policy of the State referred to in the Responsible Development Plan prepared by Prime Minister Morawiecki. Public procurement can, in fact, be one of the strongest mechanisms for implementing the economic policy of the State. The spending of large amounts of public funds can and should therefore be directed towards achieving specific objectives. Why is the amendment, not the new law, looking at the extent of this amendment? First of all, because we listened to the voice of entrepreneurs, who quite unambiguously and uniformly presented their position in order to approach the amendment. We have therefore tried to make use of solutions that already exist in the Public Procurement Law and, at the same time, meet or are not contrary to the requirements of the Directives. This brings us to the conclusion that, in the short time we have left to implement the directives, we are still using the conceptual grid and solutions that are known to us by practitioners in this space. Therefore, looking at the time that I have already mentioned, April 2016, when these directives need to be implemented, we have decided to make an effort to prepare for the revision and carry it through the process - just as the first step - to change public procurement policy. After the amendment, it was also Prime Minister Morawiecki, the Minister for Development, who announced that work on a completely new public procurement system will begin, and I stress the system and not only the law, because here both legislative and non-legislative actions will be important for this area to function efficiently and effectively. On the other hand, some of the solutions that go beyond the direct requirements of the Directive, which can be introduced during the first period, are also here. I'm about to say a few more words about them. The project, even though it is only an amendment, is still the subject of a very vivid interest of the wide range of participants in the procurement market. We got over $1,000. various types of comments to the project. A two-day agreement conference was held during which both the contracting party and the party to the contractors justified their comments. I think that much of the demands have been reflected in the next version of this project, which was addressed to the Committee. In general, the project also gathered positive evaluations in the Social Dialogue Council, except for the in-house procurement solution. Here the Social Dialogue Council opts for this solution not to be included in this amendment. On the other hand, the second important participant in the consultation process, also legally established, so to speak, i.e. the Joint Government and Territorial Government Commission, has requested that the in-house solution be implemented to the full extent that the Directive provides for. The revision, as I said, is part of the implementation of the Responsible Development Plan for the formulation of a new smart purchasing policy. The amendment therefore provides for solutions to strengthen non-price criteria for the assessment of tenders: the cost of the life cycle of the contract as a criterion for assessing tenders, reducing administrative obligations in proceedings, innovation, increasing SME participation in the procurement market and applying employment contracts and social clauses. I will briefly present what falls within the different scopes as an example of solutions, starting with non-price criteria for the evaluation of offers. Here, above all, we assume in the project that we are increasing the importance of non-price criteria in the evaluation of offers. We know that this has been under discussion for several years and has already been the subject of changes to the law in the last term, which have, so to speak, been the subject of a legal situation in the direction, I will say on a daily basis, of discouraging the application of only price criteria. However, the effects, although improved in this respect, are still not satisfactory. That's why we've proposed a mechanism here so that the price criterion can have weight. a maximum of 60% if the subject-matter of the contract is not standardised and the contracting authority does not indicate the standards in the specification of the essential terms of the contract. In this case, the price criterion is to be that threshold, a maximum of 60%. On the other hand, if the contracting authority presents these quality standards, it shall describe the subject matter of the contract by means of them, then, in that case, that level 60% It won't apply. Then it can be up to 100% - if the subject-matter of the contract is defined in a precise manner by indicating those standards so that it is a certain minimum level, such a filter allowing tenders to be tendered. Although the rules currently do not prevent the contracting authority from making criteria in this way, unfortunately statistics show that some contracting authorities are sufficiently, I would say clever, that this non-price criterion is applied at a level of 12%. Thus, they are already implementing a provision saying that in such cases the price criterion cannot be 100%. That's where the 60% threshold comes from. as a criterion for assessing offers, which should contribute to increasing the use of non-price criteria, which is quite commonly indicated as one of the main needs and demands of the market above all. If the contracting authority fails to describe the subject matter of the contract through these standards, the tenderers shall compete to a significant extent, i.e. at least within the scope of those 40% , non-price elements, i.e. de facto the quality of their offers. So this is the realization of this formula: value for money. In addition, the project will also be able to take into account the characteristics of the tender, in particular the eligibility criterion and the experience of the persons designated for the performance of the contract or the production process criterion, i.e. use of a specific process of production or execution of works. The purpose of this is to ensure that the persons who are designated by the contractor to perform the contract actually comply with the standards set by the contracting authority and that possible replacement of those persons is possible with the consent of the contracting authority. I think that such a procedural or even organisational proposal in the project is an obligation to set up a team for contracts of over EUR 1 million, so that this supervision of the performance of the entire contract is from the preparation and award of the contract to the receipt of the contract. We hope that the involvement of the same people, because this de facto is the solution, will allow you to treat the order just as a holistic process, which is particularly important part of a high quality effect, i.e. the implementation of the project. This will, in our view, make greater use of non-price evaluation criteria, because the natural tendency of those who choose offers is to choose a rather safer offer without looking at this effect. Those who receive this often later complain that the wrong offer has been chosen, which is optimal. Therefore, we want this formula to work with these large orders. Finally, as regards the departure from the price criterion as the only criterion, it is also envisaged that contracting authorities may use the cost of the life cycle as an assessment criterion. At present, the price is a mandatory criterion, while the proposal is to directly allow the use of the cost of the product lifecycle instead of the price criterion, so that it takes into account the cost of the entire life cycle of the contract, i.e. not only the cost of the purchase, i.e. the price, but also the costs, I do not know, the use, repair, disposal, recycling, etc. Again, these solutions are intended to make it possible to depart from the price criterion as the main criterion for assessing the offer. When it comes to reducing red tape, it is also one of the most important, I would say, scopes in the directive, but also in solutions that go beyond. I think that the essence of these solutions is to reduce the number of obligations for contractors to submit documents or, in particular, by tenderers. Again, these solutions will be particularly relevant to larger orders. For example, in an open invitation to tender, the contracting authority will first be able to assess the tenders and only later to request documents from the tenderer whose tender it will assess at most. In tenders above the Union thresholds, the tender or request for admission to the proceedings will be accompanied by the so-called. a single European procurement document - a solution from the Directive - which will include a statement from the contractor that it meets the conditions for participation in the procedure and is not excluded. As a rule, it is only in case of doubt as to the status of the contractor and before the award of the contract that the contracting authority will be able to request, first of all, documents confirming what is in the statements, so that the full subject-matter documentation is only here. So it's kind of a reversal of this rule of collecting documents. The point is to use mostly statements until the choice is made. There is also a proposal to introduce relatively extensive, but also, above all, more flexible provisions for amending the contract. There'll be more scope for a change of contract. Such situations will probably work first and foremost when there is a need to extend a specific public task, which often makes it necessary to choose another contractor, and, of course, in the strict regime provided for by the Act, this additional necessary action could be carried out more smoothly by amending the contract. Promotion of innovation. As two of these main actions or proposals, I would, first of all, give the contracting authority the authority to apply the criterion for assessing tenders related to innovation and, secondly, to introduce a completely new procedure for the award of public contracts, i.e. Innovation partnerships. The partnership may cover further activities, from research and development, including the search for solutions and the submission of R &amp; D projects, prototyping to product development, provision of services or completion of works. In all these areas this will be possible, i.e. for example, the contracting authorities will be able to order even non-existent technologies. and combine in one procedure various stages of research and development and even implementation. An important area where we have also tried to go beyond the Directive is to increase the possibility of SMEs participating in contracts. For example, the project rules allow for a more flexible division of orders into smaller ones, for example. the obligation for the contracting authority to justify why the contract has not been divided into smaller parts, or e.g. The introduction of an upper cap on the company's income which may be required by the contracting authority, i.e. a maximum double of the value of the order, which, again known, automatically eliminates certain barriers for smaller companies. Abolishing pre-financing constraints - in all competitive modes this will already be possible and not only limited, as it is today, to European funds, but also to national spending. Finally, the answer to a very big problem, particularly indicated by local companies, i.e. strengthening the possibility for the contracting authority to request the personal execution of key parts of the contract, i.e. the use of references. Currently, although the contracting authority requests the personal execution of the contract, such a reservation is ineffective when the contractor refers to the resources of its subcontractors in order to demonstrate the resources needed to obtain the contract. In this way, companies operate to a large extent, which are de facto limited to the organisation of sub-contractor groups, and smaller, usually local companies, which actually employ employees and have equipment, most often act as sub-contractors. We also know where this margin is highest. The aim is therefore to change this practice also through changes in the law. Then the contracting authority will be able to enforce the contractor's performance, which will naturally promote local companies, if I may say so. Another change is also the introduction of the possibility of limiting the number of parts of the contract for which one contractor can apply, or the change which, in my opinion, also improves the situation of small and medium-sized companies, i.e. the obligation to publish annual procurement plans on the contracting parties, as this gives potential tenderers the opportunity to distribute their resources, prepare their offer in view of the tenders planned by the contracting authority or the contracting authority concerned. Of course, this also increases transparency. The next issue is employment agreement and social clauses. A very important aspect of this project, i.e. the obligation for contracting authorities to require the employment of persons or staff on the basis of a contract of employment, if the nature of the work meets the definition of employment relationship within the meaning of the Labour Code, i.e. the work is carried out at a specific place and time, under the direction of the employer. This obligation will apply to both contractors and subcontractors. The Employer will indicate those jobs that qualify for employment contracts so as not to go the other way, that I will say so that where the Labour Code does not require employment on the basis of employment relationships, the Employer is obliged or could demand. So this is a balanced solution, and this is not only a bow towards employees, because it is like the first obvious effect and effect, because they are the main beneficiaries of this solution, but it is also equal to the competitive position of companies that employ their employees in accordance with the requirements of labour law. And again the rules in force, also thanks to the changes of a year or two years ago, allow the law to impose such requirements, but looking at statistics, it does not look particularly optimistic, because only a few percent of the orderers put these requirements in employment. Hence the obligation, but the obligation limited to the labour law regime. Social clauses are a particularly important aspect, I would say here, directives. For example, the law will allow contracting authorities, when implementing the directive, to reserve contracts for sheltered work establishments or contractors with at least 30% persons employed are disadvantaged persons. This is raising... today's threshold is 50%. This will also increase the number of companies that will be able to participate in restricted tenders. I would also say, in this area, that the solution is also a specific regime for the award of public contracts for social services and other specific services. Here is an increase in the thresholds below which contracts will be published only at national level, and these are service contracts e.g. health, social or cultural, which is also natural, by directing them to entities fulfilling certain conditions set out in the Act, will be very important for local companies, which are then the natural ones... are in the first series to obtain these orders. And the so-called question. in-house contracts - which, I think I can say, during the process of public consultation aroused and aroused, because the process is not completed, most discussions, such lively ones - i.e. the issue of contracts awarded by the contracting authority to subordinate and controlled entities. The draft adopted by the Council of Ministers introduces these provisions into the Public Procurement Law or proposes the introduction of these provisions into the Public Procurement Law. These are, of course, based on the provisions of the Directive, which in turn constitute a codification of the existing case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union. The directive does not have a firm obligation to implement this solution, but there is no obligation to faithfully transfer these solutions to Polish law. The directives indicate a certain maximum level that can be implemented here. The government project is trying to balance the rations and demands of the contracting representatives on the one hand and the contractors on the other. That is why we are proposing provisions that will not be a simple transposition of the provisions of the Directives in this respect. First of all, the provisions on the in-house exception will not constitute an exemption from the application of the law, which the Directive allows, but will be included as a condition for the award of a contract from a free hand. This is not a semantic or minor difference, because this means that the award of these contracts will have to be carried out in a transparent manner, as well as with strict conditions of transparency. In contrast to total disabling, the free hand mode provides for e.g. an announcement of the intention to conclude a contract, so to whom, at what price I intend to award a contract, as well as the possibility to appeal against the choice of such a procedure by the contracting authority. In relation to the directive, we also propose, on a daily basis, to increase the possibility of outsourcing tasks without a tender. First of all, according to the project, the controlled entity, i.e. the municipal company will have to carry out at least 90%, not 80%, as permitted by the Directive, of its activities in favour of the controlling contracting entity, i.e. in order to obtain the contract without a tender, i.e. in a free-of-charge manner. the parent self-government unit. The project will make it possible to apply the in-house exception in cases where a public entity orders the execution of a contract to a subsidiary whose activities are in fact almost entirely focused on the service of the contracting authority. So once again, a bit of a routine: if you want to operate in a competitive market to a large extent, do not take advantage of this exception in-house, exception or de facto in-house benefits. We also propose that, as a change to the Directive, a ban on participation in these private capital entities, with the exception of public-private partnerships and employee shares. Thus, the solution restricts the participation of those entities that will benefit from this exception or benefits will limit such direct competition with private entrepreneurs on the market. On the other hand, as a result of this solution, public entities which, in turn, have often invested significant resources themselves in the equipment of their subordinates will also be able to manage these resources through these entities or through them, through this solution. So, as I said, the project aims to balance these two divergent, very divergent positions, so that I say, both the opponents of in-house procurement in order law and the supporters of the widest possible exception under the directives. The advantage of this solution in the amendment is that we will probably increase legal certainty, because this will prevent differences in interpretation, because today we also have quite a rich case law, especially the European Court, and some contracting authorities are also acting on the basis of this case law. At the same time - I will also mention this, because this was not foreseen in the original version of the project by the Minister of Development - the project was also supplemented by amendments to the Law on the maintenance of order and cleanliness in municipalities which also assume these changes, subjecting the management of waste to the general principles of Public Procurement Law, i.e. the rules on in-house procurement. Finally, may I mention only the extension of legal protection measures primarily to the selection of the tender and the description of the subject-matter of the contract in orders below the thresholds. That's not in the rules today, that's not an option. The second point is also to increase the certainty of public procurement law and the uniformity of its application. The President of the Public Procurement Office will issue an opinion interpreting the rules if there is an important legal issue or rules that raise serious interpretation doubts or create differences in application or case law. This will be interpreted by the President of the Office as such, which will be published on the website of the Public Procurement Office. Thank you for the opportunity to present the project, I ask you to take a positive approach to the project. Of course, we are declaring full availability here as regards the work of the committee or perhaps of the subcommittee, if this is set up. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u348	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the draft law amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws, print No 366. It was precisely the erroneously acting Public Procurement Law that led to the fact that in Poland, although there were great road projects to be realized, as a result of their implementation with giants on a European scale or, even I would say, the world became some Western European companies, while Polish companies simply fell. That was the result. So that moving away from this lower price criterion is certainly a good direction, it is worth supporting. It is also worth mentioning, however, that within the framework of the previous version of the Public Procurement Law, where the price criterion was the only decisive criterion for the choice of the tenderer, under which, so to speak, we built the most expensive, if not one of the most expensive, road in the world, i.e. road S8 in Warsaw, whose cost is estimated by various sources at up to PLN 200 million per km. This is absurd. So that it is fortunate that the adoption of this amendment is about to bring about a certain time of remembrance. It is only a pity that this recollection comes from the European Union, and it is not the result of our Sejm's work. On the other hand, as regards the issue of specific provisions, there are many positive points in this bill. You can't replace them here because I only have five minutes. First of all, we pay attention to the issue of social clauses, the issue of better engagement, the extension of the circle of players who use the so-called resources. disadvantaged persons. We will certainly pay attention to this so that it is not overlooked in any way. We will also pay attention to the issue and the problem of the in-house issue that all the previous speakers have actually mentioned. Because on the one hand it is said that this law, the new Public Procurement Law, is intended to provide some kind of impetus for Polish entrepreneurs, especially Polish small and medium-sized entrepreneurs. For example, it says: Attention to the issue of reducing formal obligations. According to the impact assessment of the regulation, this project, the project designer noted that the contractors were obliged to lose in principle 4 million man-hours - I do not know whether annually or in general - on issues related to the obligation to complete all these formal cases concerning participation in the public contract. On the other hand, this unfortunate in-house is introduced, which causes in practice... It is simply a nail to the coffin for Polish entrepreneurs. Yesterday before the Sejm there was a protest of entrepreneurs from the waste management industry. I've been dating these entrepreneurs. They said straight: these records make us really lose the point of being. It is intended to be limited to elements such as environmental protection, social inclusion, employment, continuity and quality of services, promotion of innovation. This also gives rise to risks, so it is necessary to keep this law loose, but above all it is also safer. They are often companies with foreign capital, which in Poland have one office, often rented, employing several, up to dozens of administrative employees, companies without assets, aimed at profit by squeezing the seventh sweats from Polish subcontractors, from Polish small and medium-sized enterprises. These companies play from the position of force, and as a result they often lead Polish companies to financial problems. Such cases on the market, and during the bessy, and during the hossy, we have no doubt, I think they need not be cited here. The market must probably minimise the participation of such companies, but it must also significantly improve the liquidity of contracts so that subcontractors do not remain at the mercy of general clearing contractors, especially at the end of contract deadlines. That should definitely be taken into account. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The whole remodeling is needed. We have some proposals and we will be happy to join the committee or subcommittee that will be set up. It would be good not to implement such important changes in Poland at such a rapid pace, but to draw lessons from it for the future and leave some more time to work in this House. If we introduce only parts, the two parts will not meet the requirement either, and they will all be divided into two parts and not into more, because so the law, in quotes, suggests: how much and for what parts. Hence, we have adopted a different strategy, i.e. to introduce here this obligation to justify why I did not split the order, but to approach it in this time regime, which will be calmer, when preparing the new Public Procurement Law, there to think about it and for example. by even giving delegations to issue regulations, I do not know, for the construction sector, for manufacturers of metal products, to match these solutions related to division. We agree with the Senate's argument that there are disproportionately high maximum penalties to the size of the national open investment fund market. The Senate has consistently reduced sanctions for the activities of alternative investment funds. This is intended to prevent a possible drain from the investment funds industry of high-end specialists. At the same time, this is a symmetrical solution to the aforementioned monetary penalty system applicable to open investment funds. At the same time, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club expresses its negative attitude towards amendments 6, 16, 33, 37 and 41. We share the view of the Legislative Office of the Sejm about the unnecessary introduction of these amendments, and even, as in the case of Amendment 6, the introduction of amendments may give rise to interpretation doubts. In conclusion, the parliamentary club has a positive opinion on the 36 amendments of the Senate, while the 5 amendments are negative, I will repeat: 6, 16, 33, 37 and 41. Thank you very much.

To this end, I would also like to point out that at the moment there are many farms with more than 500 hectares, so it will be difficult for farmers who can have 300 hectares to compete with farmers whose farms have more than 500 hectares at the moment. We therefore call for this amendment to be adopted. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u570	Mrs. Marshal! As the rapporteur has discussed the draft in very detail, I will refer to only a few aspects. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Since 2012 there has been a geological and mining law in Poland. The introduction of this law involved the implementation by the European Commission of proceedings before the European Court of Justice in relation to the allegation that Poland had failed to implement the so-called. the hydrocarbon directive. Unfortunately, at that time the legal basis for the modification of concessions was not clarified in the provisions of the Act. The proposed amendment clarifies the issue of licensing both at the request of the trader and issued by the Authority of its own motion. The revision of the provisions of the Geological and Mining Law took place in 2014, as the rapporteur said. Since then, some problems have been observed not only with regard to the interpretation of some of the provisions introduced, but also with regard to the activities of exploration, exploration and exploitation of hydrocarbons. Carbohydrates, I will remind you, are crude oil, natural gas, their natural derivatives, as well as methane present in coal deposits, with the exception of methane occurring as accompanying mineral. An application for concessions for exploration and exploration of hydrocarbons and for the extraction of hydrocarbons from deposits may be made by any economic operator, which is issued by the Minister of the Environment. Unfortunately, the provisions of the Law amended in 2014 even prevent the conduct of proceedings in respect of the activities I mentioned. It is therefore necessary to revise the transitional provisions governing concessions awarded after the entry into force of the amended law amending the Geological and Mining Law, since today's practice indicates that due to the incorrect wording of the article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and paragraph 1. 3 there was a legal gap. There is no legal regulation for the award of concessions after 30 September 2014, but from applications submitted before the amending act, as they do not exist in the light of transitional provisions. Today's proposed change regulates this condition. We also believe that the important interest of the State, here in detail the rapporteur, has talked about this, so I will no longer develop it, is in favour of reducing the vacathio legis for the proposed project to the maximum. In view of the arguments made in this explanatory memorandum, Law and Justice welcomes the proposed bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u592	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In this statement, I would like to draw attention to the problem of the victims of World War II and the post-war period, so-called. children of war. This problem remains legally unregulated until today. During my parliamentary work, I took note of two such cases: Mrs Marianna Podgórska and Mr Stanisław Kopcia, people who need help in these matters. Marianna Podgórska from Prażma's house was born on 5 July 1942 in the village of Budziska, the district of Łukowski. Her resume is tragic. As a 2-month-old infant on 28 September 1942, she was executed together with 26 residents of the village of Budziska and Krzówka. She miraculously avoided death. Immediately thereafter, she lost both her parents: Father Wacław was arrested and murdered in 1942 in the German concentration camp Majdanek in Lublin, the mother of Bogusław died on April 25, 1945. As a 3-year-old child, Marianna Podgórska became an orphan. Since then, she has been solely on her own and the kindness of other people. These events made her childhood, youth, and life very difficult. In a short time, she lost her parents, her childhood, her health, and the possibility of a normal life. At present, Mrs. Marianna is 74 years old, ill and in need of medical attention. The monthly income he receives is at the level of the lowest pension. Despite so many unpleasant experiences, Mrs Marianna Podgórska is not a combatant, but a person to whom the provisions of the Veterans Act of 24 January 1991 apply. I would argue that Mrs Marianna's repeated efforts to obtain compensation for the traumatic experiences of World War II have not been achieved. Stanisław Kopeć, born in 1940, lost his father when he was only six months old. Mr Stanisław's father, including Stanisław, was imprisoned and consequently murdered by the Germans. Since his father's imprisonment and death, Mr. Stanisław Kopec no longer had a normal childhood and a normal life. His family was subjected to military and post-war repression as a result of his family's commitment to fighting for free and sovereign Poland. Mr Stanisław Kopec, despite his traumatic experiences, difficult childhood and youth, did not meet with the kindness of the authorities in their efforts to make amends. He is currently in a difficult health and material situation. It is maintained from the lowest pension, which is certainly not enough for a decent life, treatment and maintenance. I therefore ask the High House whether, in the light of the facts presented by me, Mrs Marianna Podgórska and Mr Stanisław Kopeć and others in a similar situation can count on the care and assistance of the Polish State. In my opinion, yes, because it is high time to lean on these problems as well. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-31-sejm-15-2.u600	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This year we celebrate the 736th anniversary of the location of Bochni. I am honored that at such an important time for Bochnia, I can look with you at the beautiful history of this city by giving the seat of a Member of the Parliament of the Republic of Poland. Bochnia is undoubtedly one of the most beautiful and oldest cities in Poland. Its location makes the southern part a hilly landscape, while the northern part occupies the plains, surrounded by the river Raba and located near the Niepołomice Forest. The first mention of the settlement called Bochnia dates back to 1198, when the then patriarch of Jerusalem Munich confirmed the giving of salt from this village for the monastery of the Miechów. Of course, it was the weighted salt obtained in Bochnia from salt sources. The breakthrough for the city was the year 1248, when the Willow Griffin officially reported the discovery of the deposits of stone salt in Bochnia. 5 years later, on 27 February 1253, the Duke of Cracow and Sandomierz Bolesław V, called the Shameful, issued an act of location in Korczyn where he allowed Bochnia to be founded on Magdeburg law with the privileges of Wrocław, whose legal arrangements became a model for Bochnia. The location of the city was a direct consequence of the discovery in the area of the Stone Salt Bochnia. The great times of the city are the times of King Casimir the Great. This excellent host organized the Bochean cottage, giving it mining ordination and expanding the cottage, in which he repeatedly visited with his manor house. In the 15th and first half of the 16th century, the city, located on important trade routes to Hungary and Russia, maintained economic and trade relations with many centres in the country. In the second half of the 16th century. Bochnia entered a period of deep crisis, which lasted throughout the 17th and 18th centuries. Later on, a period particularly difficult in history Bochnia is, of course, a period of German occupation. The Bochen post-war years are mainly significant economic development of the city, possible among others. the creation of new workplaces. The 736th anniversary of Bochni's location is not only a celebration of the city, but above all a celebration of all its residents and guests. In the case of this unique city also tourists and all visitors create a unique atmosphere. On the occasion of the city's birthday, I would like to congratulate the people of this unique place with heartfelt congratulations - you live in a special place. I wish the local authorities of Bochnia, as well as the people of Bochnia, another successful years of development. I wish that the decisions made best serve the city, and I wish Bochnia continued to attract investors, to develop dynamically. I wish all guests visiting Bochnia a pleasant time and unforgettable experience. God's blessings for the years to come! [[Applause]]
